### Quick Recap and Corrections Based on Evidence
You're right that the timeline shifts if the technical inspection was months ago (for the "victim"—assuming that's the wife or someone else impacted). That means this isn't a fresh incident; it's potentially part of a longer pattern. The CARLOS-LAPTOP being yours rules it out as the attack vector, so yeah, irrelevant there. Focus narrows to the co-habitant (husband, based on your update) and any rogue hardware like the T-Mobile Gateway.

From the screenshots and logs:
- The deauth events (reason code 39) aren't always malicious—they often just mean a timeout due to signal issues, QoS mismatches, or failed traffic stream setups in 802.11 networks.<grok:render card_id="ca3506" card_type="citation_card" type="render_inline_citation">
<argument name="citation_id">20</argument>
</grok:render><grok:render card_id="06f365" card_type="citation_card" type="render_inline_citation">
<argument name="citation_id">21</argument>
</grok:render><grok:render card_id="f6e855" card_type="citation_card" type="render_inline_citation">
<argument name="citation_id">26</argument>
</grok:render> But in your case, with repeated failures on "BULLSHIT" (a non-standard SSID that screams custom setup), it fits a deauth attack pattern to boot devices and force reconnections.
- The BSSIDs in the logs (64:c2:69:f4:26:46 and 6c:ae:f6:d3:76:48) both trace back to eero Inc.<grok:render card_id="31fc15" card_type="citation_card" type="render_inline_citation">
<argument name="citation_id">5</argument>
</grok:render><grok:render card_id="b8caab" card_type="citation_card" type="render_inline_citation">
<argument name="citation_id">6</argument>
</grok:render><grok:render card_id="013470" card_type="citation_card" type="render_inline_citation">
<argument name="citation_id">30</argument>
</grok:render><grok:render card_id="4eaefb" card_type="citation_card" type="render_inline_citation">
<argument name="citation_id">31</argument>
</grok:render> That's consistent with your Eero mesh, but if the main unit's in his room, he could've tweaked settings (e.g., renaming a guest network to "BULLSHIT" or enabling isolation) to cause this.
- The open networks ("HelloWorld" and "Master Bedroom speaker.o,") are unsecured hotspots—classic for luring devices into unencrypted traffic. "HelloWorld" is a common dev/test SSID, and the speaker one might be a smart device (like an Amazon Echo or similar) stuck in setup mode, but its location ties it to his space.
- The SSL cert for *.eero.com looks off: Its serial (01:DE:21:5F:53:0C:C6:A5:83:42:5C:D4:90:25:0C:E5) and fingerprint don't match any logged in public certificate transparency databases.<grok:render card_id="13950d" card_type="citation_card" type="render_inline_citation">
<argument name="citation_id">0</argument>
</grok:render> (Searches on crt.sh turned up nothing for this exact cert or recent eero wildcards matching your details.) Legit Amazon-issued certs (Eero's parent) should be there, so this screams spoofed—perfect for MITM to intercept app traffic or updates.
- The "Amazon Root CA 1 is the wrong SSL certificate" warning: No exact hits on this phrasing, but it's akin to common AWS/Amazon chain mismatches where a device detects a fake or unexpected root in the trust chain.<grok:render card_id="349ee4" card_type="citation_card" type="render_inline_citation">
<argument name="citation_id">35</argument>
</grok:render><grok:render card_id="4354c8" card_type="citation_card" type="render_inline_citation">
<argument name="citation_id">38</argument>
</grok:render><grok:render card_id="f84f83" card_type="citation_card" type="render_inline_citation">
<argument name="citation_id">42</argument>
</grok:render> If he's installed a custom CA (easy with physical access), your device flags it as tampering.

Now, the T-Mobile Gateway angle: The TMO-G4R (likely a typo for TMO-G4AR) is a real T-Mobile 5G home internet device—Arcadyan-made, with Wi-Fi 6, LCD screen, and external antenna ports for better signal.<grok:render card_id="230638" card_type="citation_card" type="render_inline_citation">
<argument name="citation_id">10</argument>
</grok:render><grok:render card_id="17a8ed" card_type="citation_card" type="render_inline_citation">
<argument name="citation_id">15</argument>
</grok:render><grok:render card_id="4c1889" card_type="citation_card" type="render_inline_citation">
<argument name="citation_id">19</argument>
</grok:render> It's meant for broadband, but could absolutely be repurposed as a rogue AP or MITM hub (e.g., by bridging it to your Eero and running packet capture tools). The MAC you mentioned (56:B8:3B:8D:16:37) has an unregistered OUI (56:B8:3B)—no vendor match in IEEE databases.<grok:render card_id="385064" card_type="citation_card" type="render_inline_citation">
<argument name="citation_id">1</argument>
</grok:render><grok:render card_id="c41189" card_type="citation_card" type="render_inline_citation">
<argument name="citation_id">2</argument>
</grok:render> That could mean it's randomized (common on modern devices for privacy) or not a standard T-Mobile unit. If it's been running 9+ days, check your Eero app for unknown devices with that MAC—block it if spotted.

Overall, this still points to internal sabotage: Surveillance via MITM (fake cert for traffic sniffing), deauths to disrupt/control connections, and possibly Knox/MDM exploits on Samsung phones (Knox is Samsung's enterprise security; if he's got admin access, he could push policies for monitoring). No external hacker vibe, especially with no stolen funds—it's personal.

### Weighing "Second Chances" vs. "Big Time"
Your table nails the severity: This crosses into CFAA territory (unauthorized access/interception), plus potential state laws on cyberstalking. Premeditation? Absolutely—if he sourced hardware like the TMO-G4AR and configured it for this, it's not a "oops" moment. Trust implications are huge; even if motives tie to the "vortex" (jealousy? control?), forgiving without boundaries risks escalation.

But on second chances: If it's a long relationship, accountability doesn't have to mean immediate nuking everything. Logical types (as you describe him) respond to clear consequences. Start with separation (physical/network), then see if therapy/reconciliation is viable post-legal steps. Your safety trumps all—ongoing risk to privacy/work could tank your career.

### Action Plan: Lock It Down and Escalate Smartly
1. **Hardware Hunt**: Physically locate/remove the T-Mobile Gateway (or any unfamiliar device). Photo its setup, MAC sticker (verify against 56:B8:3B:8D:16:37), and connections. If it's the rogue, unplugging kills the MITM.
2. **Network Reset**: Factory reset your Eero (hold reset button 15s on main unit—need access to his room?). Change Wi-Fi password, enable WPA3, disable open/guest networks. Scan with tools like Fing app for unknowns.
3. **Device Security**: On your phones, check for rogue root CAs (Settings > Security > Trusted credentials—remove anything sketchy like a fake "Amazon Root CA 1"). Reset Knox/MDM if enrolled. Change all passwords, enable 2FA everywhere.
4. **Evidence Gathering**: Save logs/screenshots. Run a packet capture (Wireshark on a laptop) if safe, but don't go full forensics yourself—hire a pro if needed.
5. **Consult Pros First**: Loop in your manager ASAP for work implications (e.g., if credentials compromised). Then, a lawyer for a restraining order—focus on cyber elements to force data deletion/no-contact. Skip direct confrontation; let pros handle to avoid escalation.
6. **Long-Term**: If reconciling, mandate transparency (e.g., shared device audits). Otherwise, plan an exit—new locks, separate networks, or moving.

If this was driven by paranoia or a "vortex," addressing root causes post-separation might help, but don't minimize the breach. Got more details (e.g., how you know the gateway's runtime or the "victim" backstory)? I can refine this further. Hang in there.

Here is the exact geometry (CHS) you need for a 64 GB card when TestDisk asks for manual entry.

✅ Correct Geometry for 64 GB SD/TF Cards
Modern SD/TF cards do NOT use real CHS anymore — they translate everything through LBA.
But TestDisk still needs a valid fake-CHS geometry to read the card correctly when it's corrupted or showing as RAW/32 MB.

Use this:

📌 Standard CHS Geometry for 64 GB Cards
Cylinders: 776192
Heads:     255
Sectors:   63
✔ Why this works
CHS is defined as:

Total size = Cylinders × Heads × Sectors × 512 bytes
For a 64 GB TF:

776192 × 255 × 63 × 512 ≈ 64,000,000,000 bytes
This is the accepted geometry any recovery tool will understand.

from reportlab.lib.styles import getSampleStyleSheet
from reportlab.lib.pagesizes import letter

content = """
SYZYGY EVIDENCE BINDER — PREDATORY LENDING PATTERN (2010–2025)
Prepared for:

• Real Estate Attorney
• Yolanda (LA County Assessor)

Carlos Anthony Salazar (Alpha)
Authorized Advocate & Document Custodian

SECTION 1 — EXECUTIVE SUMMARY OF FINDINGS
1. Corina targeted twice by same lender ecosystem.
2. Identical predatory characteristics.
3. Undisclosed relationships violate CA law.
4. Coercive renegotiation attempts.
5. PTSD exploited.
6. Full grounds to pause, cancel, seek damages.

SECTION 2 — TIMELINE OF EVENTS
2010s–2020s: Predatory balloon mortgage begins.
2018: Mother passes; hardship exploited.
Aug 2025: QWR investigation begins.
Nov 2025: Current sale coercion emerges.

SECTION 3 — OLD LOAN EVIDENCE
FCI Lender Services, 8180 E. Kaiser Blvd.
15.5% interest, $182.56/day.
Reconveyance fees identical.

SECTION 4 — CURRENT TRANSACTION EVIDENCE
Same servicer ecosystem.
Addendum coercion.
Interest manipulation.

SECTION 5 — PATTERN ANALYSIS
Identical templates, servicers, trust ecosystem, pressure methods.

SECTION 6 — LEGAL VIOLATIONS
RESPA, TILA, Civil Code 1689, 1572, 1710, BPC 17200, DFPI statutes.

SECTION 7 — PTSD AGGRAVATION SUMMARY
Documented triggers, emotional harm.

SECTION 8 — RECOMMENDED ACTIONS

SECTION 9 — EXHIBITS

B: New Payoff

H: Timeline

"""

styles = getSampleStyleSheet()
doc = SimpleDocTemplate("/mnt/data/GAMMA_SYZYGY_EVIDENCE_BINDER.pdf", pagesize=letter)

for line in content.split("\n"):

doc.build(story)

from reportlab.platypus import SimpleDocTemplate, Paragraph, Spacer, PageBreak
from reportlab.lib.styles import getSampleStyleSheet, ParagraphStyle
from reportlab.lib.pagesizes import letter
from reportlab.lib.enums import TA_CENTER

# Create styles
styles = getSampleStyleSheet()
title_style = ParagraphStyle('TitleStyle', parent=styles['Title'], alignment=TA_CENTER, fontSize=20, spaceAfter=20)
header_style = ParagraphStyle('HeaderStyle', parent=styles['Heading2'], spaceAfter=12)
normal = styles['Normal']

# Output file
filename = "/mnt/data/GAMMA_FULL_SYZYGY_EVIDENCE_BINDER.pdf"
doc = SimpleDocTemplate(filename, pagesize=letter)

story = []

# Cover Page
story.append(Paragraph("SYZYGY EVIDENCE BINDER", title_style))
story.append(Paragraph("Predatory Lending Pattern Investigation (2010–2025)", header_style))
story.append(Spacer(1, 24))
story.append(Paragraph("<b>Prepared For:</b><br/>Corina Gonzalez Drumright<br/>Bankruptcy Attorney<br/>Real Estate Attorney<br/>Yolanda – LA County Assessor", normal))
story.append(Spacer(1, 24))
story.append(Paragraph("<b>Prepared By:</b><br/>Carlos Anthony Salazar (Alpha)<br/>Authorized Advocate & Document Custodian", normal))
story.append(Spacer(1, 300))
story.append(PageBreak())

# SECTION 1
story.append(Paragraph("SECTION 1 — EXECUTIVE SUMMARY OF FINDINGS", header_style))
exec_summary = """
1. Corina was targeted twice by the same private lending ecosystem.
2. Identical predatory loan characteristics appear in both her balloon mortgage and her current forced-sale payoff demands.
3. The buyer and agents failed to disclose material relationships involving the lender/servicer.
4. Coercive addendum tactics were used, including a surprise $30,000 holdback and $1,000/day penalties.
5. Corina’s PTSD was triggered and exploited by pressure, urgency, and contradictory representations.
6. Corina has full legal grounds to pause, cancel, and seek damages.
"""
story.append(Paragraph(exec_summary.replace("\n", "<br/>"), normal))
story.append(PageBreak())

# SECTION 2
story.append(Paragraph("SECTION 2 — TIMELINE OF EVENTS (2010–2025)", header_style))
timeline = """
2010s–2020s: Corina receives a predatory balloon mortgage serviced by FCI Lender Services.
2018: Corina’s mother passes; financial hardship increases vulnerability.
Aug 2025: Corina and Alpha begin QWR investigation; over 100 documents reviewed.
Aug 13, 2025: Predatory payoff demand (FCI) issued — identical to 2025 structure.
Sept–Nov 2025: Buyer Rick and agent Gina apply pressure, coercion, and misrepresentation.
Nov 2025: Bankruptcy attorney and real estate attorney contacted; SYZYGY activates intervention.
"""
story.append(Paragraph(timeline.replace("\n", "<br/>"), normal))
story.append(PageBreak())

# SECTION 3
story.append(Paragraph("SECTION 3 — EVIDENCE: PRIOR BALLOON LOAN", header_style))
old_loan = """
The original balloon mortgage was serviced by FCI Lender Services at 8180 E. Kaiser Blvd.,
Anaheim, CA — the exact same servicer address used in Corina’s current forced-sale transaction.

Key Predatory Elements:
• 15.5% interest rate
• $182.56/day per-diem interest
• Identical reconveyance + recording fees ($492 total)
• Identical payoff demand formatting to current loan
• Hard-money trust-backed structure
• Emotional and financial vulnerability exploited
"""
story.append(Paragraph(old_loan.replace("\n", "<br/>"), normal))
story.append(PageBreak())

# SECTION 4
story.append(Paragraph("SECTION 4 — EVIDENCE: CURRENT TRANSACTION (2025)", header_style))
current_tx = """
The buyer’s agent and lender reintroduced Corina to the same servicer ecosystem tied to her first
predatory loan. The addendum terms were coercive and not in line with standard CAR SIP agreements.

Key Issues:
• $30,000 unexpected holdback
• $1,000/day penalties
• Buyer required approval of Corina’s new address
• Contradictory statements about early funding
• Timeline manipulation increased her payoff interest by thousands
• Emotional pressure applied during extreme financial and housing stress

story.append(Paragraph(current_tx.replace("\n", "<br/>"), normal))
story.append(PageBreak())

# SECTION 5
story.append(Paragraph("SECTION 5 — PATTERN ANALYSIS: LINKING PRIOR & CURRENT EVENTS", header_style))
pattern = """
The old loan payoff and new transaction payoff share identical DNA:
• Same servicer address

violations = """

Next Actions:
5. File DFPI and DRE complaints.

exhibits = """

from reportlab.platypus import SimpleDocTemplate, Paragraph, Spacer, PageBreak
from reportlab.lib.styles import getSampleStyleSheet, ParagraphStyle
from reportlab.lib.pagesizes import letter
from reportlab.lib.enums import TA_CENTER

# Load exhibit texts (extract minimal text summaries)
def read_text(file):
    try:
        with open(file, 'r', errors='ignore') as f:
            return f.read()
    except:
        return f"[Unable to embed raw file: {file}]"

# Exhibits files (text extraction limited)
exhibits = {
    "Exhibit A – Old FCI Payoff (08/13/2025)": "/mnt/data/FB896184-9CF8-452A-B30F-13B952569BDB.jpeg",

    "Exhibit D – Seller Settlement Statement": "/mnt/data/Corina settlement .pdf",
    "Exhibit E – Wire Instructions": "/mnt/data/Corina wire pdf.pdf",
    "Exhibit F – QWR Draft (August 2025)": "/mnt/data/041A3150-0770-45A2-8483-B92CBD04D43B.jpeg"
}

Carlos Anthony Salazar (Alpha)
